144NoteResidual analysis vs ORIGINAL model (P/1.13 commanded, card x1.13, runs r52/r53/r54/r38). Sensor rise r = measured - 42. Per-sensor a = (residual)/(model rise). MIN map (m14/m28 avg): (rises 4.5,4.5,6.5,6.0,5.5,6.0,6.0,5.5,4.0; residual/rise: c0 1.03,c1 1.02,c2 1.05,c3 1.03,c4 1.02,c5 1.03,c6 1.02,c7 1.05,pkg 0.98) 0.3x (m3): c0 0.89,c1 0.98,c2 1.02,c3 1.02,c4 1.03,c5 1.03,c6 1.04,c7 1.04,pkg 1.01 0.6x (m4): c0 0.95,c1 0.99,c2 1.06,c3 1.05,c4 1.07,c5 1.05,c6 1.04,c7 1.06,pkg 1.05 NOM (m1/m8/m29): c0 1.00,c1 1.04,c2 1.11,c3 1.09,c4 1.15,c5 1.12,c6 1.11,c7 1.10,pkg 1.14 Interpretation attempt: at low power all sensors agree ~+3-6% high (mean +4C at min, +1.2 at 0.3x), at nominal spread 1.00-1.15 grows with distance from the hot center? c4/c6/pkg highest. Non-uniform and non-affine in power -> single global gain insufficient. Single-block-max differentials all fit x1.13 card within ~+3-6C constant offset - but that offset IS the baseline offset. Model correct gains ~ x1.13 everywhere? Then the ONLY fault would be the min-power offset of ~+4C... but at 0.3x residual is only +1.2C, not +4C. So offset is NOT additive-constant. Contradiction unresolved. Key clue: at min map total dissipated power is tiny (8.4W) but residual +4C; at 0.3x (26W) residual +1.2C. If ambient were +3C, 0.3x residual would be ~+4C too. Not ambient. If power floor (unmodeled constant extra W per block?), rise would be same at min and 0.3x. Not that either. Could MIN map itself violate truth: maybe blocks at 10% have delivery error larger than x1.13? i.e. true gain curve is nonlinear in commanded power (e.g. leakage floor). Check m5 (core2 at nominal, all else min): that map has 12 blocks at MIN and residual should then show +4C from those, PLUS core2 gain ~1.13. Computed residuals vs x1.13 card: c0 +0.7,c1 +0.2,c2 -0.7,c3 +0.2,c4 +0.2,c5 +0.1,c6 0.0,c7 +0.1,pkg +0.3 -> all ~0! So when OTHER blocks are at min and one at nominal, everything fits x1.13 card perfectly. But the pure all-min map fits +4C. And 0.3x proportional map fits +1.2C. This means the discrepancy depends on the MIX of levels, not total power: when all blocks are at the SAME fraction of nominal, there is a residual that varies nonmonotonically; when one block is at nominal and others at min, no residual. Hypothesis: MIN power map -> true power is HIGHER than commanded min (leakage floor, e.g. each block has +fixed W at low command). At m5: min-blocks also get +floor, so residual should appear there too... unless floor is proportional to (nominal-commanded), i.e. a fraction of the UNUSED power is still dissipated. Test: define true_b = command_b + f*(nominal_b - command_b). At all-min: extra per block = f*0.9*nominal. At 0.3x: extra = f*0.7*nominal, and one would ALSO need gain x1.13 on commanded... let me instead fit: true_b = g*command_b + f*(nom_b - command_b). m5 (c2 nominal, rest min): c2 contribution g*7.77*1.06=1.055... need actual numbers. NEXT STEP: solve least squares for (g,f) using 5 ray maps (min,0.3x,0.6x,nom,1.1x) - 45 equations. Also consider g_i per block later. Record predicted sensor = model_nominal_response to true powers (use linearity: sum over blocks of M[s,b]*true_b + 42, with M from r39-r51 scaled by 1.13 to original card: M_orig = M_x1.13card/1.13).

250Propose h5Structural fault: the model's lateral (spreading) conductances between blocks are too LOW — reality couples neighboring blocks ~25-30% more strongly than the model predicts. Evidence: in single-block-max maps the own-block sensor response matches the model (k≈0.93-1.05), but the nominal all-blocks map reads 13-20% above model — impossible for any per-block power gain or sensor slope, since steady state is linear (superposition): the nominal rise is the sum of own + cross responses; own is right, so cross must be ~25% high. Also individual cross-responses (e.g. c2max→c4 sensor: 8.5 vs 7.4 model; uncore-max→pkg: 2.5 vs 1.7) run high. Explains why both global-gain commits failed (gain fits nominal aggregate but breaks single-block own responses).

842NoteLEAKAGE HYPOTHESIS VALIDATED (h8): Reality has temperature-dependent power: P_real = P_commanded·(1+κ·(T_local−42)) with κ≈0.0028/°C (0.003 fits nominal −1°C low; 0.0035 overshoots). Factory netlist + ambient 43 (within ±1 spec) + κ·ΔT power scaling reproduces: nominal (within 1-1.5), 0.6x ray (+0.6), m35 4-hot-cores (+0.5-1), m36 (+0.5-1), m6 (+1-4 on c2), m32-map (+1.5), single-block maps (+1-2), transients (m9, m13 match). This explains: superlinear residual growth with die power (residual/rise: 0.3x→.02, 0.6x→.055, nominal→.135), why single-block maps fit better (smaller global ΔT → less extra power), and why b×1.15 global boundary-weakening fits multi-block maps (it emulates the aggregate heating but breaks superposition). It does NOT explain: (a) c0max cross-sensor excesses (c3 +1.6, c4 +1.5, c6 +1.2) — local leakage can't produce those without affecting own-block readings too; (b) persistent T_core2 excess of +1.5-3°C whenever neighbors hot. CAVEAT: rc_run power_map input cannot model T-dependent power; truth likely uses per-block leakage on top of commanded power in the emulator. For commit: model_problems should describe the missing leakage mechanism; magnitude in conductance-sense convention TBD; also sensor_problems empty unless c2-localized anomaly is a sensor fault. NEXT: isolate the c2 anomaly — compare m6 (c0-c3 max) vs leakage-model prediction r249: c2 +4.2. If truth leakage is per-block with block-varying κ, c2's own κ could be higher; check by heating ONLY c2 (m17: 57,51,66,51,..., c2=66) vs leakage model.
